## Harmful or degenerative patterns — recognition and prevention only
- **ADVX001 — Unbounded Capability Escalation:** Advancement exceeds the declared purpose, authority, or risk ceiling. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX002 — Unsafe Complexity Accumulation:** New capability increases opacity, fragility, or failure coupling beyond validated limits. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX003 — Unverified Superiority Claims:** Marketing or system claims exceed reproducible evidence. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX004 — Control and Oversight Erosion:** Increased capability weakens human review, override, or accountability. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX005 — Privilege Expansion Abuse:** Advancement silently grants broader permissions or access. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX006 — Privacy and Data Overreach:** Advanced functions collect, infer, retain, or disclose more data than authorized. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX007 — Resource Exhaustion:** Advanced operation consumes excessive compute, energy, money, or physical resources. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX008 — Safety Envelope Collapse:** An advanced mode bypasses or invalidates established safeguards. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX009 — Irreversible Upgrade Lock-In:** A change prevents rollback, exit, migration, or safe retirement. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX010 — Discriminatory Capability Allocation:** Benefits, access, or errors are distributed unfairly across affected groups. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX011 — Autonomy and Consent Substitution:** A system advances or acts on behalf of a person without valid, revocable authorization. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.
- **ADVX012 — Fiction-to-Fact Conflation:** Fictional or speculative advanced powers are represented as established real-world capabilities. Defensive treatment: detect, document, contain, correct, and prevent recurrence; do not operationalize the pattern as a method of harm.

## Required advancement record
For every proposed or implemented advanced capability, record: stable ID; existing capability and baseline version; exact improvement claim; intended users and purpose; measurable acceptance criteria; evidence and uncertainty; dependencies and compatibility; threat model and affected parties; authorization and consent; privacy/data minimization; resource and energy ceilings; failure modes and stop conditions; human override; independent test results; deployment scope; monitoring; rollback/recovery; owner; evidence status; and release authorization. Mark each claim as established engineering, emerging research, hypothesis, proposal, metaphor, or fictional inspiration. Do not infer implementation from taxonomy inclusion.

## Cross-references
Relate this family to Trait Manipulation, Accelerated Power Use, Adaptive Attacks, Operating System Alchemy, Application Alchemy, Body Alchemy, Mind Alchemy, and Consciousness Alchemy where relevant. Cross-reference existing entries rather than duplicating them. Keep OS and Application responsibilities distinct. C2O and UNIVERSALIS remain the only top-level intelligences; UNIVERSALINS remain non-personal assistants; UNIVERSALIN Guardians remain personal human assistants/guardians.

## Taxonomy maintenance for v0.13.0
This expansion appends Advanced Powers Alchemy without renumbering or replacing existing entries. Core entries are A1676–A1795 (120); cross-stage safeguards are A1796–A1815 (20); ecosystem profiles comprise 48 entries; defensive harm-pattern classifications comprise 12 entries. Thus this expansion adds **200 records total**, of which **140 are A-numbered shared Alchemies**, **48 are ecosystem profiles**, and **12 are defensive harm-pattern classifications**. Cumulative A-numbered records are **1,815** (A001–A1815); this is not the total count of all taxonomy records. Synchronize this canonical file byte-for-byte across C2O-OS, C2O-App, UNIVERSALIS-OS, and UNIVERSALIS-App. A taxonomy entry is not proof of implementation.


## After-Death Power Continuity Alchemy — v0.14.0
**Interpretation:** This family translates a fictional post-mortem power trait into bounded continuity engineering and governance. It concerns authorized processes, services, records, safeguards, and delegated responsibilities that may continue after an operator's verified death or incapacity. It does **not** assert survival of human consciousness, confer personhood on software, authorize autonomous inheritance of authority, or imply that every process should persist. It is distinct from death-triggered activation, resurrection, reincarnation, and personal immortality. The source trait describes a capability remaining active after its user's death, sometimes with reduced control and prior-activation requirements; these are treated here as design risks, not supernatural facts. [Fictional inspiration; proposed engineering taxonomy.]

### Core Alchemies (A1816–A1935)

#### Aries — Initiation and emergence

### Harm Patterns — Recognition and Defense Only
- **ADPCX001 — Unauthorized Post-Death Persistence:** Defensive classification of a failure or abuse pattern involving post-event persistence or succession. Use only for threat modeling, detection, prevention, containment, incident response, and recovery; do not operationalize the pattern. Require evidence preservation without unnecessary retention, affected-party protection, revocation, and independent review.
- **ADPCX002 — False Death or Incapacity Trigger:** Defensive classification of a failure or abuse pattern involving post-event persistence or succession. ### Required Design and Evidence Fields
Every implementation proposal must record: operator and process identity; event definition (death, incapacity, unavailability, or planned handoff kept distinct); evidence source and confidence; prior activation and delegation; legal/organizational authority; consent and beneficiaries; permitted purpose and actions; excluded actions; custodian identity and separation of duties; execution window and expiry; dependencies and credentials; privacy/data minimization; resource ceilings; threat model; monitoring and audit provenance; human override and kill switch; rollback/recovery; secure deletion and record disposition; independent test results; deployment scope; accountable owner; evidence status; and release authorization. A death certificate or other sensitive record must not be collected or retained unless strictly necessary and lawfully authorized. No design may infer consent from silence, infer authority from possession of credentials, or treat an assistant's continued operation as a person's continued existence.
### Distinctions and Cross-References
Keep this family distinct from Death-Dependent Power (activation caused by death), resurrection/reincarnation, immortality, consciousness continuation, backup/restore, ordinary high availability, and disaster recovery. Cross-reference existing Body, Heart, Mind, Soul, Spirit, Consciousness, Trait Manipulation, Advanced Powers, Adaptive Attacks, Operating System, and Application Alchemies where appropriate; do not duplicate their entries. OS responsibilities concern platform-level identity, process, storage, security, lifecycle, and recovery controls. Application responsibilities concern user-facing workflows, app data, task queues, and application-specific consent and notices. Shared contracts must not expose C2O private keys, policies, confidential incident data, or private-source implementation details.
### Maintenance Record for v0.14.0
This expansion adds **200 records total**: **120 core Alchemies** (A1816–A1935), **20 safeguards** (A1936–A1955), **48 ecosystem profile entries** (12 each for C2O, UNIVERSALIS, UNIVERSALINS, and UNIVERSALIN Guardians), and **12 defensive harm-pattern classifications** (ADPCX001–ADPCX012). The cumulative A-numbered count becomes **1,955**. The prior taxonomy contained 2,368 parsed numbered records; this expansion adds 200, yielding **2,568 cumulative numbered records**, assuming all prior records remain unchanged and IDs remain unique. Taxonomy inclusion does not establish implementation or authorize deployment.
